Subject: Submission of Notice of 28th Annual General Meeting of Ceinsys Tech Limited 
scheduled on Saturday, September 26, 2026, at 11:30 AM (IST) through Video Conferencing 
(VC)/Other Audio Visual Means (OAVM) along with Annual Report for the Financial Year 
2025-26. 
 
Dear Sir/Madam, 
 
Pursuant to Regulation 30 and 34 of SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015, please find enclosed Notice of 28th Annual General Meeting (AGM) and 
Annual Report for FY 2025-26 of the Company sent today i.e. on September 4, 2026, to the 
members of the Company through electronic mode whose e-mail addresses are registered with 
the Company / RTA / Depository Participant(s).  
 
The 28th AGM of the Company is scheduled to be held on Saturday, September 26, 2026, at 11:30 
AM (IST) through Video Conferencing (VC)/Other Audio Visual Means (OAVM) in compliance 
with the applicable provisions of the Companies Act, 2013, read with MCA Circulars to transact 
the Ordinary Businesses, as set out in the Notice of the AGM.  
 
Please note that the Notice convening the 28th AGM and the Annual Report for the FY 2025-26 of 
the Company are also uploaded on the Company’s website at www.cstech.ai  . 
 
 Further, pursuant to Regulation 36(1)(b) of the Securities and Exchange Board of India (Listing 
Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015, a letter providing the web-link of 
the Annual Report, is being sent to those members who have not registered their e-mail addresses. 
 
The Cut-off date for determining eligibility of Members to vote on the resolutions set out in the 
Notice of AGM by remote e-voting or by e-voting at the AGM and to attend the AGM has been 
fixed as Saturday, September 19, 2026.

NOTICE
NOTICE IS HEREBY GIVEN THAT THE 28TH ANNUAL 
GENERAL MEETING OF THE MEMBERS OF CEINSYS TECH 
LIMITED WILL BE HELD ON SATURDAY , SEPTEMBER 
26, 2026 AT 11:30 A.M. (IST) THROUGH VIDEO 
CONFERENCING (‘VC’) / OTHER AUDIO-VISUAL MEANS 
(‘OAVM’), TO TRANSACT THE FOLLOWING BUSINESS:
ORDINARY BUSINESS
Item No. 1 – Adoption of Audited Standalone Financial 
Statements
To receive, consider and adopt the Audited Standalone 
Financial Statements of the Company for the Financial Year 
ended March 31, 2026, together with the Reports of the 
Board of Directors and the Auditors thereon.
Item No. 2 – Adoption of Audited Consolidated Financial 
Statements
To receive, consider and adopt the Audited Consolidated 
Financial Statements of the Company for the Financial 
Year ended March 31, 2026, together with the Report of 
the Auditors thereon.
Item No. 3 – Declaration of Dividend
To declare Final Dividend of `3.50/- (Three Rupees Fifty 
Paisa Only) per Equity Share of `10/- each for the Financial 
Year ended March 31, 2026.
Item No. 4 – Appointment of Director, who retires by 
rotation.
To appoint a Director in place of Mr. Kaushik Khona (DIN: 
00026597), who retires by rotation in terms of Section 152 
(6) of the Companies Act, 2013, and being eligible, seeks 
re-appointment.
By order of the Board of Directors for
	
CEINSYS TECH LIMITED

16.	 Members may note that pursuant to the Finance Act, 
2020, dividend income is taxable in the hands of the 
Shareholders effective April 1, 2020 and the Company

with TDS requirements, Members are requested to

Company / RTA by sending documents through e-mail.
	
A Resident individual shareholder with PAN and who 
is not liable to pay income tax can submit a yearly 
declaration in Form No. 15G/ 15H, to avail the benefit 
of non- deduction of tax at source by email tds@
bigshareonline.com by 11:59 PM (IST) on Saturday, 
September 19, 2026. Shareholders are requested to 
note that in case their PAN is not registered, the tax 
will be deducted at a higher rate of 20%.
	
Non-resident shareholders can avail beneficial rates 
under tax treaty between India and their country of 
residence, subject to providing necessary documents 
i.e. No Permanent Establishment and Beneficial 
Ownership Declaration, Tax Residency Certificate, Form 
10F, any other document which may be required to 
avail the tax treaty benefits by sending an email to tds@
bigshareonline.com. The aforesaid declarations and 
documents need to be submitted by the shareholders 
by 11:59 PM (IST) on Saturday, September 19, 2026.
17.	 Pursuant to the provisions of Sections 124 and 125 of

transferred by the Company to the Investor Education

THE INSTRUCTIONS FOR MEMBERS FOR REMOTE 
E-VOTING AND JOINING ANNUAL GENERAL 
MEETING ARE AS UNDER:-
The remote e-voting period begins on Wednesday, 
September 23, 2026 at 9:00 A.M (IST) and ends on Friday, 
September 25, 2026 at 5:00 P.M. The remote e-voting 
module shall be disabled by NSDL for voting thereafter. The 
Members, whose names appear in the Register of Members 
/ Beneficial Owners as on the record date (cut-off date) i.e. 
September 19, 2026 may cast their vote electronically. The 
voting right of shareholders shall be in proportion to their 
share in the paid-up equity share capital of the Company as 
on the cut-off date, being September 19, 2026.
How do I vote electronically using NSDL e-Voting system?
The way to vote electronically on NSDL e-Voting system 
consists of “Two Steps” which are mentioned below:
Step 1: Access to NSDL e-Voting system
A)	
Login method for e-Voting and joining virtual meeting for 
Individual shareholders holding securities in demat mode
In terms of SEBI circular dated December 9, 2020 
on e-Voting facility provided by Listed Companies, 
Individual shareholders holding securities in demat 
mode are allowed to vote through their demat 
account maintained with Depositories and Depository 
Participants. Shareholders are advised to update their 
mobile number and email Id in their demat accounts 
in order to access e-Voting facility.

Login method for Individual shareholders holding securities in demat mode is given below:
Type of shareholders
 Login Method
Individual 
Shareholders holding 
securities in demat 
mode with NSDL.
1.	
For OTP based login you can click on  https://eservices.nsdl.com/SecureWeb/evoting/
evotinglogin.jsp. You will have to enter your 8-digit DP ID,8-digit Client Id, PAN No., Verification 
code and generate OTP. Enter the OTP received on registered email id/mobile number and 
click on login. After successful authentication, you will be redirected to NSDL Depository site 
wherein you can see e-Voting page. Click on company name or e-Voting service provider i.e. 
NSDL and you will be redirected to e-Voting website of NSDL for casting your vote during 
the remote e-Voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting.
2.	
Existing IDeAS user can visit the e-Services website of NSDL Viz. https://eservices.nsdl.
com either on a Personal Computer or on a mobile. On the e-Services home page click on 
the “Beneficial Owner” icon under “Login” which is available under ‘IDeAS’ section , this will 
prompt you to enter your existing User ID and Password. After successful authentication, you 
will be able to see e-Voting services under Value added services. Click on “Access to e-Voting” 
under e-Voting services and you will be able to see e-Voting page. Click on company name 
or e-Voting service provider i.e. NSDL and you will be re-directed to e-Voting website of NSDL 
for casting your vote during the remote e-Voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting 
during the meeting.
3.	
If you are not registered for IDeAS e-Services, option to register is available at https://
eservices.nsdl.com. Select “Register Online for IDeAS Portal” or click at https://eservices.
nsdl.com/SecureWeb/IdeasDirectReg.jsp
4.	
Visit the e-Voting website of NSDL. Open web browser by typing the following URL: https://
www.evoting.nsdl.com/ either on a Personal Computer or on a mobile. Once the home 
page of e-Voting system is launched, click on the icon “Login” which is available under 
‘Shareholder/Member’ section. A new screen will open. You will have to enter your User ID (i.e. 
your sixteen digit demat account number hold with NSDL), Password/OTP and a Verification 
Code as shown on the screen. After successful authentication, you will be redirected to NSDL 
Depository site wherein you can see e-Voting page. Click on company name or e-Voting 
service provider i.e. NSDL and you will be redirected to e-Voting website of NSDL for casting 
your vote during the remote e-Voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the 
meeting.
5.	
Shareholders/Members can also download NSDL Mobile App “NSDL Speede” facility by 
scanning the QR code mentioned below for seamless voting experience.

Brief Profile 
Mr. Kaushik Khona, is a Chartered Accountant, Company Secretary, Cost 
and Management Accountant with 37 years plus of experience in varied 
Industries including Aviation, Travel, Forex, Facility management services, 
Machinery manufacturing, Agro- Bio fertiliser- Tissue culture and Veterinary 
products, Shipping, Rubber tyre, Agro processed Value added Products & 
Wind Mills sector with in depth exposure in devising and implementing 
business strategies, Turn-around Management, Business Reengineering 
and Business operations, Finance & Accounts, Tax Management and 
administration, Budgeting-MIS, Systems implementation.
He is an Enterprising leader & planner with a strong record of contributions 
in 
streamlining 
operations, 
invigorating 
businesses, 
heightening 
productivity, reducing costs, implementing systems and procedures.
In addition to above Mr. Khona is well versed with a business environment 
of different economies and has been instrumental in formulating strategic 
plans for growth & sustainability of the business, M&A, Strategic Buy Out, 
Sell Off and Business Reengineering. Currently, he is serving as Director 
and Managing Director, India Operations of the Company.

A TRIBUTE
Late Shri Datta Meghe was a towering figure in Maharashtra’s public life as a 
statesman, educationist, and social leader whose contributions to the region spanned 
more than five decades of dedicated service. 
The Meghe Group, which he founded and led with vision and purpose, carries forward 
the values of integrity, service, and the relentless pursuit of progress that he embodied.
At CS TECH Ai, we are privileged to be part of the legacy he built. His guidance, values, 
and unwavering commitment to building a better Maharashtra remain the foundation 
upon which this Company continues to grow.

algorithms are not merely tools, but strategic enablers, 
shaping the future of business at scale.

ABOUT US
CS TECH AI DELIVERS HIGH-IMPACT SOLUTIONS FOR 
GOVERNMENTS, SMART CITIES, UTILITIES, MOBILITY, 
AND INDUSTRIAL ASSETS.
Established in 1998 under the 
Meghe Group, Ceinsys Tech Limited 
was built on a mission to solve 
complex, real-world problems 
through the power of technology. 
Over 27 years, the Company earned 
a strong reputation in geospatial 
intelligence and enterprise 
solutions, bringing data-driven 
precision to infrastructure, utilities, 
and natural resource projects. 
Today, CS TECH Ai is listed on both 
BSE and NSE, with a pure-play 
focus across the interconnected 
domains of geospatial services, 
technology solutions, and mobility 
engineering. Together, these 
create a geo-mobility value chain 
that spans end-to-end GIS data 
services, enterprise Web GIS and 
decision support systems, full-
vehicle product engineering, and 
industrial automation.

DEAR SHAREHOLDERS,
It is with immense pride that I 
present the Annual Report for 
FY2025-26. This year stands as the 
most defining chapter in CS TECH 
Ai’s 27-year journey.
Before I speak of the year’s 
performance, I wish to acknowledge 
a profound loss. Our founder, Late 
Shri Dattatraya Raghobaji Meghe, 
passed away on 22 March 2026 at 
the age of 89. His values of integrity, 
service, and relentless progress are 
carried forward in everything the 
Meghe Group does and we remain 
committed to honouring him through 
the quality of our work.
When I reflect on what this Company 
has built over the past several 
years, what strikes me most is the 
consistency and conviction that has 
brought us here. In a market where 
many players have struggled with 
slow procurement cycles, funding 
constraints, and delayed decision-
making, CS TECH Ai has consistently 
delivered positive revenue growth, 
maintained zero client churn 
across 100+ active projects, and 
strengthened every significant metric 
on our balance sheet.
THE YEAR GONE BY
FY2025-26 was a year of exceptional 
financial performance. Operational 
revenue grew 58% year-on-year to 
INR 6,607 million. EBITDA rose 86% 
to INR 1,449 million, with margins 
expanding 327 basis points to 21.9%. 
Profit After Tax grew 111% to INR 
1,334 million, with PAT margins 
crossing 20% for the first time in the 
Company’s history, an expansion 
of 507 basis points year-on-year. 
Diluted EPS stood at INR 67.87, 
reflecting a 92.4% increase over the 
previous year.
The balance sheet strengthened 
significantly. Net cash stood at INR 
2,480 million as on 31 March 2026, 
nearly double the INR 1,225 million of 
the prior year. The closing order book 
of INR 8,760 million provides strong 
revenue visibility into the year ahead. 
Geospatial and Engineering Services 
grew 76% year-on-year to INR 
3,588 million. Technology Solutions 
grew 41% to INR 3,009 million. Both 
segments delivered expanding 
margins alongside revenue growth.
NEW INITIATIVES
FY2025-26 was a year of significant 
strategic action alongside strong 
operational delivery. On 19 February 
2026, CS TECH Ai achieved a major 
capital market milestone with the 
listing of its equity shares on the 
National Stock Exchange of India. 
This dual-listing on both BSE and 
NSE broadens our investor base, 
improves share liquidity, and 
reinforces our commitment to 
governance and transparency.
On the technology front, the 
Company established a dedicated 
vertical for artificial intelligence, 
machine learning, and embedded 
electronics. Two important emerging 
sub-domains are also being 
developed: Satellite and Data as a 
Service, and Mining Intelligence. 
Both are supported by strong policy 
tailwinds in India, and both represent 
meaningful long-term growth 
opportunities for a company with CS 
TECH Ai’s technical positioning.
Most recently, the Board approved a 
joint venture with AI Fabrik Inc., a US-
based artificial intelligence company. 
This is a significant development 
in the Company’s international 
expansion and AI capability agenda. 
The Board has, at present, approved 
investment of INR 25 Cr into the JV 
and completion of due diligence and 
preparation/drafting of the SPA. The 
JV is expected to create value across 
our global client base in the years 
ahead.
The Company’s inorganic growth 
strategy continues to compound 
capability. Following the acquisition 
of AllyGrow Technologies in 2022 
and VTS in 2024, the Company 
mobilised INR 2,380 million in 2025 
for further acquisition, capability 
enhancement, and international 
expansion. Additional acquisitions are 
planned across geospatial, mobility, 
and technology domains.
DELIVERY EFFECTIVENESS
CS TECH Ai’s delivery model is 
its most enduring competitive 
advantage. The Company maintained 
100+ active project executions with 
zero client churn across the year, a 
result of technical depth, delivery 
discipline, and relationships built on 
consistent performance.
Domestically, a major INR 41 
crore contract from MHADA for an 
integrated project management and 
GIS-based digital platform, a multi-
year subscription renewal with ISRO, 
and continued execution under the 
Jal Jeevan Mission reinforced the 
Company’s standing as a trusted 
technology partner to India’s most 
important institutions. Repeat 
engagements with SWSM, MRSAC, 
NHAI, and CIDCO reflect the durability 
of these relationships. Internationally, 
an INR 21 crore order for the Georgia 
Land Information System and 
growing momentum through the US 
subsidiary in the geospatial telecom 
sector demonstrated the reach of the 
Company’s capabilities beyond India.
Working capital management was 
equally disciplined. The net working 
capital cycle improved to 157 days. 
The Company delivered positive cash 
flow from operations, with net cash 
nearly doubling over the course of 
the year.
OUTLOOK FOR THE YEAR 
AHEAD
CS TECH Ai enters FY2026-27 with a 
strong order book, an improving cash 
position, and a pipeline expected 
to convert meaningfully in in FY27. 
Several opportunities delayed by 
code of conduct constraints over the 
past twelve months are now showing 
positive traction. The focus for the 
year FY27 will be to convert several 
opportunities which are presently 
in the funnel and are proposed to 
[result] into award of such contracts 
to build a stronger order book.
The strategic priorities for the year 
ahead are clear. The Company will 
deepen domain expertise across

SATELLITE AND DATA  
AS A SERVICE (DAAS)
MINING INTELLIGENCE
Satellite and Data as a 
Service (DaaS) is an emerging 
focus area for CS TECH Ai, 
positioned at the intersection 
of geospatial intelligence and 
India’s rapidly evolving space-
tech ecosystem. The National 
Geospatial Policy 2022 
mandates that high-accuracy 
geospatial data be owned 
and processed by Indian 
entities, effectively reserving 
a large, protected market for 
domestic players and creating 
a natural moat against 
foreign competition. This is 
Mining Intelligence is an 
emerging sub-domain for CS 
TECH Ai, targeting one of the 
most persistent governance 
challenges in the natural 
resources sector. Illegal 
extraction, route diversions, 
falsified grades, and 
unreported production cost 
governments and legitimate 
operators billions annually. 
CS TECH Ai’s Mining 
Intelligence platform digitises 
and secures the entire mining 
lifecycle, from exploration 
to post-extraction. Satellite 
imagery, LiDAR, and AI-
further reinforced by initiatives 
including PM GatiShakti, IN-
SPACe, Make in India, and Digital 
India, all of which are driving 
demand for satellite, drone, 
and LiDAR-based data across 
infrastructure, agriculture, 
disaster management, and 
urban planning. 
The end-to-end value chain, 
spanning data acquisition, 
analysis, curation, dissemination, 
and usage, offers multiple entry 
points for specialised players, 
with government departments 
and large public infrastructure 
based geological intelligence 
enable accurate reserve 
estimation at the outset. At the 
point of extraction, smart IoT 
weighbridges, RFID, and ANPR 
technology track production in 
real time. GPS-based vehicle 
tracking with geo-fenced 
corridors and route-deviation 
alerts follows minerals in 
transit. Quality and volume are 
independently verified through 
third-party grade authentication 
and audited weighbridge 
records. An ESG monitoring 
layer tracks environmental 
impact across the excavation 
projects serving as reliable, 
high-volume customers. 
With geospatial adoption 
still in its early stages across 
India’s government and 
infrastructure ecosystems, 
this sub-domain represents 
meaningful long-term growth 
potential for companies with 
the right technical capability 
and regulatory positioning. CS 
TECH Ai is building towards 
this opportunity
lifecycle. A digital twin and 
IoT governance layer ties 
the entire system together, 
comparing actual extraction 
against approved volumes to 
flag illegal mining and revenue 
leakage in real time. 
 
The result is a shift from 
paper-based, opaque 
processes to an end-to-end, 
verifiable digital trail, bringing 
transparency, accountability, 
and measurable revenue 
protection to one of India’s 
most critical and underserved 
sectors.

Annual Report 2025-26
FY26 IN RETROSPECT
The Company delivered its highest 
annual consolidated revenue to 
date, while profitability grew faster 
than revenue as the business mix 
continued to move towards higher-
value engineering and technology 
solutions.
•	
Consolidated revenue from 
operations increased 58% to 
`660.7 crore
•	
EBITDA increased to 
approximately `146 crore, with 
the full-year margin expanding 
to around 22%*
•	
Profit after tax increased 111% 
to `133.4 crore
•	
PAT margin increased to 20.2%
•	
Net worth strengthened to 
approximately `673 crore
Scaling Execution Across Core 
Businesses
Growth during the year was broad-
based. Revenue from Geospatial & 
Engineering Services increased 76% 
to `358.8 crore, while Technology 
Solutions grew 41% to `300.9 crore. 
The performance reflected increased 
execution across infrastructure, 
water, geospatial, digital engineering 
and technology-led assignments, 
together with a growing proportion of 
integrated solution delivery.
The progression through FY26 
was also marked by sequential 
improvement in profitability. 
EBITDA margins increased from 
19.4% in Q1 to 23.6% in Q4. This 
improvement was supported by 
greater participation in higher-value 
solution engagements, enhancement 
of delivery capabilities and employee 
productivity, and increasing 
application of emerging technologies 
within existing domain offerings.
Strategic Wins Strengthen Digital 
Infrastructure Portfolio
A defining feature of FY26 was the 
Company’s increased participation in 
programmes combining engineering, 
geospatial intelligence, enterprise 
platforms and digital project 
management.
Among the major assignments 
secured during the year was a 
`114.98 crore MMRDA contract for 
system integration and monitoring 
of infrastructure projects through 
the Integrated Data and Dashboard 
Platform. The Company also 
secured a `41.26 crore MHADA 
assignment for an Integrated Project 
Management System, 3D Urban 
Spatial Digital Model and web-based 
GIS platform.
Further MMRDA assignments 
included approximately `19.61 crore 
for urban change detection and BIM 
implementation and `15.95 crore for 
an Esri ArcGIS Enterprise Agreement. 
These engagements reinforced the 
Company’s position across GIS, BIM, 
digital monitoring and integrated 
infrastructure-management 
environments.
The Company also strengthened 
its engagement with large private-
sector infrastructure enterprises 
during FY26. CS TECH Ai secured 
two assignments from the Adani 
Group aggregating `7.79 crore, 
spanning implementation of a 
Common Data Environment across 
nine Energy Engineering businesses 
and the Cement business, together 
with the supply and installation of 
Autodesk Build Enterprise Licenses 
for the Group’s CDE requirements. 
The engagements demonstrate the 
Company’s growing capabilities 
in enterprise-wide digital project 
collaboration, information 
management and technology 
implementation for complex 
infrastructure organisations.
The year also saw continued traction 
across utilities and enterprise 
technology. Existing rural water-
supply inspection and monitoring 
engagements with SWSM Uttar 
Pradesh were extended, with 
the extended-duration work 
estimated at `107.07 crore across 
three clusters. CS TECH Ai also 
secured new water and wastewater 
consultancy assignments, including 
a `12.21 crore STP DPR and 
Project Management Consultancy 
engagement in Vasai–Virar.
In the private sector, the Company 
secured CDE and enterprise-
software engagements with the 
Adani Group spanning energy, 
cement and wider infrastructure 
requirements, while an ISRO service 
order extended a long-standing 
technology relationship through a 
three-year subscription renewal.
Technology-Led Expansion
FY26 also marked a deeper focus on 
emerging technology as an enabler 
of the Company’s existing domain 
capabilities. CS TECH Ai initiated 
a vertical focused on artificial 
intelligence, machine learning and 
embedded electronics, with an initial 
emphasis on developing applications 
and solutions for use across its 
established infrastructure, geospatial 
and engineering businesses.
The Company’s technology 
architecture increasingly combines 
established OEM ecosystems 
with open-source and internally 
developed applications. During the 
year, development work progressed 
on proprietary AI-enabled solutions, 
with management reporting that 
two IPs had already been filed for 
protection and were being applied 
in proof-of-concept and commercial 
environments.
This technology-led direction was 
also reflected in the Company’s 
partnership with Tech Mahindra, 
announced in January 2026, 
to jointly pursue geospatial, AI-
driven and digital-twin solutions 
for infrastructure and enterprise 
customers across international 
markets.
Building International Market 
Readiness
The Company continued to 
develop the international platform 
established through its earlier 
acquisition of the VTS geospatial 
business in the United States. During 
FY26, resources were deployed 
towards business development, 
technology creation and market 
development, particularly in North 
America. Management reported 
FY26 turnover of approximately `7–8 
crore from VTS while continuing to 
build the operation for future scale.
CS TECH Ai also entered into a 
strategic MoU with Singapore-
based Aetosky during the year to 
jointly pursue Enterprise Geospatial 
and AI-led opportunities across 
international markets. Together with 
the Tech Mahindra partnership, 
these initiatives widened the 
Company’s channels for participation 
in global infrastructure digitisation 
programmes.
Mobility and Product Engineering
The Mobility and Product Services 
businesses continued to provide a 
complementary run-rate revenue 
base outside the Company’s 
conventional project order book. 
Management indicated that these 
businesses together contribute 
approximately `150 crore of top line 
and operate predominantly through 
recurring customer commitments 
rather than large project-based 
orders.
During FY26, the Company continued 
to strengthen capabilities in 
embedded technologies and related 
engineering areas, supporting its 
broader strategy of combining 
mechanical and manufacturing 
engineering with electronics, digital 
technologies and software-led 
solutions.
BUSINESS HIGHLIGHTS

Operational and Financial Discipline
Alongside strong revenue and profit 
growth, CS TECH Ai continued to 
focus on its balance sheet and 
working-capital position. The net 
working-capital cycle stood at 157 
days at the end of FY26, improving 
from 162 days at the end of the 
preceding quarter. Management’s 
measure of overall net cash 
increased to approximately `248 
crore at March 31, 2026, compared 
with approximately `122.5 crore a 
year earlier.
Working capital nevertheless 
remained an area of continued focus 
as project billing remained linked 
to achievement and certification 
of contractual milestones. The 
Company’s scale-up during the 
year resulted in an increase in trade 
receivables and unbilled revenue, 
with management expecting billing 
conversion as project milestones are 
completed.
The balance sheet was further 
strengthened through conversion 
of outstanding share warrants 
during the year. At March 31, 2026, 
`235.05 crore of proceeds from 
the preferential issuance remained 
available in bank deposits and 
current accounts, providing financial 
flexibility for future strategic 
initiatives.
Capital Markets and Organisational 
Development
FY26 also marked an important 
milestone in the Company’s capital-
market journey, with CS TECH 
Ai’s equity shares being listed 
on the National Stock Exchange 
of India from February 19, 2026, 
complementing its existing BSE 
listing and broadening accessibility 
to investors.
The finance leadership was 
strengthened during the year with 
the appointment of CA Amita Saxena 
as Chief Financial Officer with effect 
from October 1, 2025.
Looking Ahead
CS TECH Ai entered FY27 with a 
closing disclosed order book of 
`876 crore, excluding annual and 
other customer commitments in 
Mobility and Product Services. 
Management continues to pursue 
opportunities across transportation 
infrastructure, urban development, 
energy, geospatial platforms and 
digital engineering while also 
expanding the role of AI, digital twins 
and proprietary technology within its 
solutions portfolio.
Momentum continued after the close 
of FY26. In April 2026, a consortium 
including CS TECH Ai received an 
approximately `20.85 crore contract 
for the Georgia Land Information 
System, marking an important 
international geospatial engagement. 
In May, the Company’s US subsidiary 
received an approximately `4.07 
crore order for beta development 
of a Hybrid Power Transfer Case, 
strengthening its North American 
mobility-engineering presence. 
In July, the Company received a 
`67.04 crore Letter of Intent from the 
Directorate of Urban Administration 
& Development, Madhya Pradesh for 
work under PMAY(U).
With a stronger balance sheet, an 
increasingly technology-intensive 
portfolio and a growing opportunity 
pipeline across infrastructure and 
international markets, the Company’s 
priorities for FY27 remain focused 
on conversion of the business funnel 
into executable contracts, disciplined 
project delivery, expansion of 
international operations and 
development of higher-value digital 
and IP-led solutions.

1.	
FINANCIAL PERFORMANCE
	
Revenue – Standalone
On a standalone basis in FY 2025-26, our Revenue 
from operations has shown an increase of 58.90% 
which resulted increase of Revenue to ` 63,517 lakhs 
in FY 2025-26 as compared to previous FY 2024-25 
which was ` 39,973 lakhs.
	
Revenue – Consolidated
On consolidated basis in FY 2025-26, our Revenue 
from operations has shown an increase of 58.04% 
which resulted increase of Revenue to ` 66,070 Lakhs 
in FY 2025-26 as compared to FY 2024-25 which was 
` 41,806 lakhs.
	
Profits – Standalone
On a standalone basis in FY 2025-26, the profit before 
tax and exceptional items has shown an increase of 
65.83% in current FY 2025-26 and stood at ` 18,376 
lakhs and Profit after exceptional items and tax stood 
at ` 15,421 lakhs. Whereas, in FY 2024-25 the profit 
before tax and exceptional items stood at ` 11,081 
lakhs and Profit after exceptional items and tax at 
` 8,138 lakhs.
	
Profits – Consolidated
On consolidated basis in FY 2025-26, the profit before 
share of profit of joint venture, exceptional items and 
tax has shown an increase of 84.20% and stood at 
`14,535 lakhs in FY 2025-26. profit after exceptional 
items and tax is `13,342 lakhs in FY 2025-26. Whereas, 
in FY 2024-25, the profit before share of profit of joint 
venture exceptional and tax stood at ` 7,891 lakhs and 
Profit after exceptional items and tax at ` 6,324 lakhs.
	
EPS- Standalone
On a standalone basis, the Basic Earnings Per Share 
increased to ` 86.41 for FY 2025-26 as compared 
to Basic EPS of `48.09 for last FY 2024-25. The 
substantial improvement in Basic EPS was primarily 
driven by the Company’s strong operational and 
financial performance during the year, reflecting 
higher profitability and enhanced value creation for 
its shareholders.
	
EPS- Consolidated
On a consolidated basis, the Company’s Basic Earnings 
Per Share (EPS) increased significantly to `74.76 for 
FY 2025–26, as compared to Basic EPS `37.37 for FY 
2024–25. The substantial increase in Basic EPS reflects 
the improved consolidated financial performance and 
enhanced profitability achieved by the Company 
during the year.
2.	
DIVIDEND
The Board of Directors have recommended a final 
dividend of `3.50 per equity share for the FY 2025-
26 aggregating to `732.82 lakhs, subject to approval 
of Shareholders at the ensuing 28th Annual General 
Meeting (“AGM”) of the Company.

13.	 RISK MANAGEMENT
The Company has sound systems to manage the risks. 
Management of Risk is an integral and important 
component of Corporate Governance. This robust Risk 
Management system ensures adequate controls and 
monitoring mechanisms for a smooth and efficient 
running of the business.
The Company’s overall Risk Management Framework 
helps in identifying risks and opportunities that may 
have a bearing on the organization’s objectives, 
assessing them in terms of likelihood and magnitude 
of impact and determining a response strategy. This 
framework seeks to create transparency, minimize 
adverse impact on the business strategy and enhance 
the Company’s competitive advantage. This risk 
management system thus helps in managing market, 
credit and operational risks and quantifies potential 
impact at a Company level. Management Discussion 
& Analysis Report contains detailed information on 
the risk management process of the Company.
The Company is not required to constitute Risk 
Management Committee as provided in the SEBI 
(Listing Obligations & Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015.
14.	 BUSINESS DESCRIPTION AND STATE OF 
COMPANY’S AFFAIRS
Ceinsys Tech Limited operates at the forefront of 
technology-driven solutions, specializing in geospatial 
services, industrial automation, and infrastructure 
development. As a leader in the industry, Ceinsys excels 
in delivering tailored solutions that empower clients 
across various sectors to optimize their operations and 
achieve sustainable growth. The Company’s state of 
affairs reflects robust growth and a commitment to 
innovation, evidenced by its strategic expansions into 
new markets and pioneering projects that established 
industry benchmarks. Ceinsys’ steadfast dedication to 
quality, reliability, and customer satisfaction underpins 
its success, making it a trusted partner for organizations 
seeking transformative solutions in today’s dynamic 
business landscape.
Ceinsys remains committed to enhancing enterprise-
wide resilience and aims to set the standard for 
business excellence. Our ongoing investments in 
strategic initiatives underscore our dedication to 
deliver enhanced outcomes for our clients.

Currently, the services offered by Ceinsys, and its 
subsidiaries encompass a diverse range of areas:
•	
Geospatial Services and solutions cover mapping 
of cadastral terrain, natural resources like mining, 
forest, and agriculture, as well as health, sanitation, 
enterprise assets, and utilities.
•	
Web GIS Integrated solutions include decision 
support systems, enterprise administrative 
portals, and end-to-end capabilities in Utilities, 
Municipal, ULBS and agriculture domains.
•	
Expert engineering solutions provide consulting 
services for Non-Revenue Water (NRW), urban 
and rural water supply and sewage schemes 
(DPR), SCADA and automation for water and 
electric transmission and distribution, smart 
water solutions, and metering Infrastructure 
(AMI). Additionally, services extend to Roads 
and Highways (DPRs), Road Asset Management 
System (RAMS), corridor mapping, 5DBIM, CDE 
for Transport, Infrastructure, and Construction 
industry, Project Management Consultancy (PMC) 
and Third-Party Inspection (TPI).
•	
New-age technology solutions encompass 
Lidar Data Capturing and Processing, UAV Data 
capturing and processing, Laser Scanning and 3D 
Modelling, Point cloud extraction, Photogrammetry 
Solutions, Digital Twin Engineering, and AI/ML-
based solutions.
•	
Automotive Engineering Services include 
product engineering such as concept generation, 
engineering, detail design, feasibility studies, 
packaging, integration, virtual verification 
through CAE/FEA/CFD, and change management. 
Manufacturing engineering covers consulting, 
plant design and optimization, tooling design, 
robotics automation, and simulation. Advanced 
technologies focus on IoT and IIOT for Industry 
4.0 and AI-based computer vision solutions.
Ceinsys is actively expanding its capabilities and 
establishing itself as an integrated solution provider 
in Geospatial, Engineering, Automotive, and IT sectors.
During the financial year 2025-26, there has been no 
change in the nature of business of the Company and 
no significant change in the nature of business of its 
subsidiaries.
15.	 HUMAN CAPITAL MANAGEMENT
The Company firmly believes that its people are the 
cornerstone of its long-term growth, innovation, 
operational excellence, and sustainable value 
creation. The Company continues to strengthen its 
human capital foundation by attracting, nurturing, 
and retaining highly skilled, future-ready, and value-
driven professionals across business functions and 
technology domains. As on March 31, 2026, the 
Company’s total workforce stood at 1104 employees. 
The Human Resources function continues to play a 
strategic role in enabling business transformation 
by aligning talent management practices with the 
Company’s growth objectives, operational priorities, 
customer expectations, and governance standards. The 
Company’s people philosophy is centered on building 
an agile, high-performance, and learning-oriented 
organization that fosters accountability, innovation, 
collaboration, inclusion, and continuous professional 
development.
The Company remains focused on creating a workplace 
culture that empowers employees, strengthens 
leadership capability, encourages continuous learning, 
and promotes employee well-being while maintaining 
high standards of ethics, integrity, and corporate 
governance. Through structured HR interventions, 
employee-centric initiatives, and digital enablement 
of people processes, the Company continues to 
enhance workforce productivity, engagement, and 
organizational effectiveness.
	
Employee Engagement and Employee Experience
The Company recognizes that employee engagement 
and employee experience are critical drivers of 
organizational performance, retention, innovation, 
and customer satisfaction. Accordingly, the Company 
continues to invest in strengthening employee 
engagement frameworks and creating a positive, 
collaborative, and inclusive work environment that 
supports both professional and personal growth of 
employees.
The Company’s HR policies, people processes, and 
engagement initiatives are continuously reviewed 
and aligned with evolving business requirements and 
workforce expectations. Various employee engagement 
programs are conducted throughout the year based 
on structured feedback mechanisms, leadership 
interactions, employee surveys, periodic connect 
sessions, and organization-wide communication 
platforms. These initiatives are aimed at improving 
employee satisfaction, strengthening organizational 
culture, fostering collaboration across teams, and 
enhancing overall employee morale and productivity.
The Company has continued its focus on employee 
well-being through initiatives encompassing work-
life balance, mental wellness awareness, team 
collaboration, leadership accessibility, recognition 
programs, and employee participation in organizational 
activities. Structured recognition and reward practices 
continue to encourage high performance, innovation, 
ownership, and contribution toward business 
objectives.
The Company also emphasizes transparent 
communication and leadership connect initiatives 
to strengthen trust, alignment, and organizational 
cohesiveness across all levels. Through regular 
engagement forums and people-centric interventions, 
the Company continues to reinforce a culture of 
openness, inclusivity, and mutual respect.
	
Learning, Development and Capability Building
The Company firmly believes that continuous 
learning and capability development are essential to 
sustaining competitive advantage in a rapidly evolving 
technology and business environment. Accordingly, 
the Company has established a robust learning and 
development ecosystem focused on strengthening 
technical expertise, functional capability, leadership 
effectiveness, and future-readiness of its workforce.
During the year, the Company underwent external 
appraisal and review processes under the CMMI 
framework, wherein the Company’s training 
governance, competency development framework, 
learning management practices, and capability-
building initiatives were positively acknowledged for 
adoption of mature and industry-recognized best 
practices. The Company continues to maintain strong 
focus on process maturity, organizational capability 
enhancement, and knowledge-driven operational 
excellence aligned with CMMI Level 5 standards.
The Company has embedded structured learning 
objectives into its annual performance management 
framework through defined Goals and Key Result Areas 
(KRAs), including mandatory learning hours applicable 
across employee levels and functions. This approach 
reinforces a culture of continuous professional 
development and demonstrates the Company’s 
strategic investment in strengthening organizational 
capability and long-term talent sustainability.
The competency-based development framework 
enables employees to enhance domain expertise 
and acquire future-ready skills aligned with evolving 
customer, business, and technology requirements. The 
Company’s skill index methodology and competency 
mapping mechanisms were well appreciated during 
external appraisal assessments for their structured 
and measurable approach toward workforce capability 
enhancement.
The Company actively promotes a self-directed learning 
culture wherein employees are encouraged to pursue 
learning at their own pace through digital learning 
platforms, leadership mentoring, knowledge-sharing 
forums, certification programs, specialized technical 
training, and role-based development interventions. 
Senior leaders and functional experts continue to play 
an active role in mentoring and guiding employees in 
their professional development journeys.
The Company’s centralized knowledge repository and 
learning ecosystem currently comprise professional 
certifications delivered across multiple technical, 
functional, behavioural, compliance, and leadership 
domains during the year. The Company continues 
to leverage digital learning platforms and learning 
applications to improve accessibility, scalability, 
and effectiveness of training initiatives across the 
organization.
	
Performance Management and Talent Development
The Company continues to strengthen its performance-
driven culture through a structured performance 
management framework aligned with business 
strategy, operational objectives, and individual 
accountability. The performance management process 
emphasizes goal alignment, measurable outcomes, 
competency enhancement, continuous feedback, and 
employee development.
Annual goals and KRAs are designed to ensure 
alignment between organizational priorities and 
individual contributions while encouraging ownership, 
collaboration, and innovation. The Company continues 
to focus on identifying high-potential talent, 
strengthening succession readiness, and enabling 
leadership development through targeted capability-
building initiatives and managerial interventions.
The Company also places strong emphasis on internal 
mobility, cross-functional exposure, and role-based 
progression opportunities to support career growth 
and talent retention. The leadership team remains 
actively engaged in fostering a culture of meritocracy, 
accountability, and continuous improvement across 
the organization.
	
Inclusion, Diversity and Equal Opportunity
The Company is committed to fostering an equitable, 
inclusive, and diverse workplace that promotes equal 
opportunity, fairness, dignity, and respect for all 
individuals. The Company believes that diversity of 
thought, experience, background, and perspective 
contributes significantly toward innovation, 
organizational agility, better decision-making, and 
sustainable business growth.
The Company’s Diversity and Non-Discrimination 
Policy guides its people management practices, talent 
acquisition processes, workplace conduct standards, 
and employee engagement initiatives to ensure an 
inclusive and discrimination-free work environment. 
Employment-related decisions continue to be based 
on merit, competency, performance, and organizational 
requirements without discrimination of any nature.
The Company remains committed toward supporting 
persons with disabilities and enabling meaningful 
workplace participation through inclusive employment 
practices and supportive work environments. Focused 
efforts undertaken in this area have resulted in 
successful employee integration and retention 
outcomes.
As an Equal Opportunity Employer, the Company 
continues to strengthen initiatives that encourage

Human Rights, Ethics and Workplace Practices

that ethical workplace practices, fair employment

form the foundation of a responsible and sustainable

The Company continues to maintain policies and 
governance mechanisms aimed at ensuring a 
safe, secure, respectful, and harassment-free work 
environment for all employees. The Company promotes 
ethical conduct, transparency, accountability, and

Outlook

resilient, digitally enabled, and future-ready workforce 
capable of supporting the organization’s strategic 
growth ambitions and evolving customer requirements. 
Going forward, the Company will continue to focus 
on strengthening talent capability, leadership

The Company believes that sustained investment in

17.	 SUBSIDIARIES, ASSOCIATES AND JOINT 
VENTURE
During the financial year 2025-26, Hon’ble National 
Company Law Tribunal (“NCLT”), Bench at Mumbai vide 
its Order dated April 11, 2025 (“Order”), had approved 
Scheme of Amalgamation in the form of Merger 
by absorption (“Scheme”) of Allygrow Technologies 
Private Limited, the Transferor Company (Wholly 
Owned Subsidiary Company of the Company) with 
Ceinsys Tech Limited, the Transferee (Company). The 
Appointed Date of the Scheme was April 1, 2024. The 
Scheme came into effect on April 30, 2025, pursuant to 
filing of form INC-28 by both the Transferor Company 
and Transferee Company.
As a part of the Company’s growth strategy and in order 
to expand Company’s operations globally, the Board 
of Directors of the Company at their meeting held on 
November 11, 2025, granted an in-principle approval 
for expanding Company’s Operations Globally by 
establishing Wholly Owned Subsidiary at the Kingdom 
of Saudi Arabia (KSA).
As on March 31, 2026, the Company has following 
seven direct Subsidiaries:
1.	
ADCC Infocom Private Limited (Wholly Owned 
Subsidiary);
2.	
Ceinsys Tech (Singapore) Pte Ltd (Wholly Owned 
Subsidiary);
3.	
Allygrow Technologies B.V. (Wholly Owned 
Subsidiary);
4.	
Technology Associates Inc. (Wholly Owned 
Subsidiary);
5.	
Allygrow Technologies GmbH (Wholly Owned 
Subsidiary);
6.	
Allygrow Technologies UK Limited (Wholly Owned 
Subsidiary);
7.	
Allygram Systems and Technologies Private 
Limited (JV Subsidiary Company).
As on March 31, 2026, Allygram Systems and 
Technologies Private Limited was identified as Material 
Subsidiary of the Company as per the thresholds laid 
down under the Listing Regulations and Policy for 
determining material subsidiaries which is in line with 
the Listing Regulations as amended from time to time. 
The web link of the Policy for determining material 
subsidiaries is given in Annexure-6 of this Board 
Report.
Pursuant to provisions of Section 129(3) of the 
Companies Act, 2013, the Company has prepared 
Consolidated financial statements of the Company and 
its subsidiary, which form part of the Annual Report. 
Further, a statement containing the salient features of 
the financial statement of the Company’s subsidiaries 
in the prescribed format AOC-1 is appended as an 
Annexure-1 to the Board’s Report.
In accordance with the third proviso of Section 136(1) 
of the Companies Act, 2013, the Annual Report of 
the Company, containing therein its Standalone and 
Consolidated financial statements have been placed 
on the website of the Company at www.cstech.ai.
18.	 CORPORATE GOVERNANCE
The Company has adopted best corporate governance 
practices, which are consistently followed and 
strengthened over time. The Company believes that 
good governance is the basis for sustainable growth 
of the business and for enhancement of stakeholder’s 
value. The directors of the Company reaffirm their 
continued commitment to good corporate governance 
practices. It is an ethically driven business process 
that is committed to values aimed at enhancing an 
organization’s brand and reputation. This is ensured 
by taking ethical business decisions and conducting 
business with a firm commitment to values, while 
meeting stakeholders’ expectations. A detailed report 
on Corporate Governance forms an integral part of 
this Annual Report and is set out as separate section 
therein.

designed to ensure reliability of financial reporting, 
compliance with policies, procedure, applicable laws 
and regulations and that all assets and resources are 
acquired economically, used efficiently and adequately 
protected.

controls were adequate and effective during the 
financial year 2025-26. For more details, refer to the

31.	 CORPORATE SOCIAL RESPONSIBILITY
The Company is committed to conduct its business 
in a socially responsible, ethical and environmentally 
friendly manner and to continuously work towards 
improving quality of life of the communities in its 
operational areas. The Board of Directors at its meeting 
held on July 26, 2014, had constituted Corporate 
Social Responsibility (CSR) Committee in accordance 
with Section 135 of Companies Act, 2013 read with 
rules formulated therein. The CSR agenda is planned 
in consultation with the CSR committee members 
through a systematic independent need assessment. 
Your Company believes in positive relationships 
that are built with constructive engagement which 
enhances the economic, social and cultural well–
being of individuals and regions connected to the 
Company’s activities. The Company has adopted a 
detailed policy on Corporate Social Responsibility. The 
brief outline of the Corporate Social Responsibility 
(CSR) Policy of the Company including the annual 
report on the Company’s CSR activities are set out 
in Annexure-5 of this report in the format prescribed 
in the Companies (Corporate Social Responsibility 
Policy) Rules, 2014.
32.	 SECRETARIAL STANDARDS
The Company duly complies with all applicable 
secretarial standards issued by the Institute of 
Company Secretaries of India.
33.	 VIGIL MECHANISM (WHISTLE BLOWER POLICY)
In terms of the section 177(9) of companies act, 2013 
and rules framed thereunder read with Regulation 22 
of Listing Regulations, your Company has established 
a ‘Vigil Mechanism Policy’ and it provides a channel to 
the employees to report to the appropriate authorities 
concerns about unethical behaviour, actual or 
suspected, fraud or violation of the Company’s code 
of conduct policy and provides safeguards against 
victimization of employees who avail the mechanism 
and also provide a direct access to the Chairman of 
the Audit Committee in exceptional cases. Protected 
disclosures can be made by a whistle blower through 
an e-mail, or telephone line or a letter to the concerned 
authorities. The web link of the vigil mechanism 
(whistle blower policy) is given in Annexure-6 of this 
Board Report.
34.	 PREVENTION OF INSIDER TRADING
The Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) 
vide notification dated January 15, 2015, had put in 
place a framework for prohibition of Insider Trading 
in securities and to strengthen the legal framework 
thereof. These regulations of the SEBI under the 
above notification had become effective from May 15, 
2015. Whereas on December 31, 2018, the regulations 
were amended with Securities and Exchange Board 
of India (Prohibition of Insider Trading) (Amendment) 
Regulations, 2018 effective from April 1, 2019. 
Accordingly, the Board has formulated Code of 
Conduct and Fair Disclosure for Prevention of Insider 
Trading Policy in accordance with Regulation 8 & 9 
of Securities and Exchange Board of India (Prohibition 
of Insider Trading) Regulations, 2015 for regulating, 
monitoring and reporting of Trading of Shares by 
Insiders. The Code lays down guidelines, procedures 
to be followed and disclosures to be made while 
dealing with shares of the Company. The details of the 
Code of Conduct and Fair Disclosure for Prevention 
of Insider Trading are given in Annexure-6 of this 
Board’s Report.

The designation of Mr. Sagar Meghe was changed from Non-Executive Non-Independent Director cum Chairman to Whole Time Director and Chairman of the Company 
with effect from December 16, 2025. The remuneration mentioned above is paid in the capacity of Whole Time Director and Chairman of the Company w.e.f. December 
16, 2025.
3.	
Mr. Prashant Kamat resigned from the post of Whole Time Director, Vice Chairman & CEO of the Company with effect from December 31, 2025 and was re-designated 
as Non-Executive Non-Independent Director of the Company with effect from January 1, 2026. The remuneration mentioned above was paid in the capacity of Whole 
Time Director, Vice Chairman & CEO of the Company upto December 31, 2025.
4.	
During the year, Mr. Surej Kunhithayyil Poyil was appointed as Additional Director (Executive), CEO Designate of the Company and CEO of the Wholly Owned Subsidiary. 
Subsequently, he was appointed as Non-Executive Non-Independent Director and later as Whole-Time Director & CEO Designate, subject to requisite approvals. The 
appointment as Whole-Time Director was subsequently withdrawn. Thereafter, he resigned as Whole-Time Director and subsequently as Non-Executive Non-Independent 
Director and CEO Designate of the Company, followed by his resignation as CEO of the Wholly Owned Subsidiary. However, during the reporting period, the remuneration 
was drawn from the Wholly Owned Subsidiary Company of the Company.
5.	
Mr. Samir Sabharwal resigned from the post of Chief Financial Officer of the Company with effect from September 15, 2025.
6.	
CA Amita Saxena was appointed as Chief Financial Officer of the Company with effect from October 1, 2025.
7.	
Mr. Vishal Pawar resigned from the post of Chief Marketing Officer of the Company with effect from December 19, 2025.

I further report that
•	
The Board of Directors of the Company is duly 
constituted with proper balance of Executive Directors, 
Non-Executive Directors and Independent Directors. 
The changes in the composition of the Board of 
Directors that took place during the period under review 
were carried out in compliance with the provisions of 
the Act.
•	
Adequate notice is given to all directors to schedule 
the Board Meetings, agenda and detailed notes on 
agenda were sent at least seven days in advance or 
when there is UPSI at least 48 hours in advance, and 
a system exists for seeking and obtaining further 
information and clarifications on the agenda items 
before the meeting and for meaningful participation 
at the meeting.
•	
Majority decision is carried through while the dissenting 
members views are captured, if any and recorded as 
part of the minutes.
I further report that as per the explanation given to me and 
the representations made by the Management and relied 
upon by me there are adequate systems and processes in 
the company commensurate with the size and operations 
of the company to monitor and ensure compliance with 
applicable laws, rules, regulations and guidelines.
I further report that during the audit period the Company 
has not undertaken any specific events/actions that 
can have a major bearing on the Company’s compliance 
responsibility in pursuance of the above referred laws, rules, 
regulations, guidelines, standard, etc., except as follows :
Allotment of 4,00,000 Equity Shares of ` 10/-
During the period under review the Company has allotted 
4,00,000 Equity Shares of ` 10/- each pursuant to exercise of 
ESOP by the eligible employee under the Ceinsys Employee 
Stock Incentive Scheme 2024 on 14/07/2025.
Allotment of 30,96,515 Equity Shares upon Conversion 
of Share Warrants
The Company at its EGM held on 29-04-2024 had obtained 
prior approval of Shareholders by way of special resolution 
for 1. Issue of 14,89,086 Share warrants and 12,50,658 
Equity Shares on Preferential basis to certain Non‐Promoters 
and 2. Issue of 16,07,429 Share Warrants on a Preferential 
basis to certain persons under promoter and promoter 
group of the Company at an issue price of ` 559.90. The 
Company pursuant to Board approval dated 20-09-2024 
made allotment of 11,01,749 Equity Shares and 30,96,515 
Convertible Share Warrants on preferential basis.
Further, during the period under review the Company 
pursuant to Board approval dated 18-03-2026 made 
allotment of 30,96,515 Equity Shares pursuant to conversion 
of the 30,96,515 Share warrants to certain non-promoter 
and persons under promoter and promoter group of the 
Company.
During the period under review the Company at its 27th AGM 
held on 29-09-2025 had obtained approval of Shareholders 
by way of special resolution for implementing ‘Ceinsys Tech 
Limited Employee Stock Option Scheme 2025’ (“CS Tech 
ESOS 2025/Scheme”) and Extension of the said Scheme 
for the Employees of the Subsidiaries/Associates/Group 
Company(ies).
I further report that the company has received merger 
order dated 11/04/2025 from Hon’ble National Company 
Law Tribunal, Mumbai Bench (“NCLT”) for merger of 
Allygrow Technologies Private Limited, the Transferor 
Company (Wholly Owned Subsidiary Company of Ceinsys 
Tech Limited) with Ceinsys Tech Limited, the Transferee 
(Company) and order has filed in form INC-28 on April 30, 
2025 by the Company.

Dear Sir / Madam,
I have conducted the secretarial audit of the compliance 
of applicable statutory provisions and the adherence to 
good Corporate Governance practice by Allygram Systems 
and Technologies Private Limited (hereinafter called “the 
Company”). Secretarial Audit was conducted in a manner 
that provided me a reasonable basis for evaluating the 
corporate conducts / statutory compliances and expressing 
our opinion thereon.
Based on my verification of the Company’s books, papers, 
minute books, forms and returns filed and other records 
maintained by the Company, the information to the extent 
provided by the Company, its officers, agents and authorized 
representatives during the conduct of secretarial audit, 
the explanations and clarifications given to me and the 
representations made by the Management and considering 
the relaxations granted by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs 
warranted due to the spread of the COVID-19 pandemic, I 
hereby report that in my opinion, the Company has during 
the audit period covering the financial year ended on 31st 
March, 2026, complied with the statutory provisions listed 
hereunder and also that the Company has proper Board 
processes and compliance mechanism in place to the 
extent, in the manner and subject to the reporting made 
hereinafter.
I further report that maintenance of proper and updated 
books, papers, minutes books, filing of forms and returns 
with applicable regulatory authorities and maintaining 
other records is the responsibility of management and of 
the Company. My responsibility is to verify the content 
of the documents and returns produce before me, make 
objective evaluation of the content in respect of compliance 
and report thereon.
I have examined on test basis, the books, papers, minute 
books, forms and returns filed and other records maintained 
by the Company and produced before me for the financial 
year ended March 31, 2026, according to the provisions of:
(i)	
The Companies Act, 2013 and the rules made there 
under;
(ii)	
The Securities Contracts (Regulation) Act, 1956 
(‘SCRA’) and the rules made there under; (to the extent 
applicable since the company has become deemed 
public Limited and desire to dematerialize their 
shareholding).
(iii)	 The Depositories Act, 1996 and the Regulations and 
Bye-laws framed there under; (to the extent applicable 
Since the company desire to dematerialize their 
shareholding).
(iv)	 Foreign Exchange Management Act, 1999 and the 
rules and regulations made thereunder to the extent of 
Foreign Direct Investment, Overseas Direct Investment 
and External Commercial Borrowings, and to the extent 
the same was applicable to the Company;
(v)	
Since the status of the Company is unlisted Private 
Limited Company, the provisions of Securities and 
Exchange Board of India Act, 1992 (‘SEBI Act’) and rules 
and regulations made there under is not applicable 
to the Company, however since the company has 
become material subsidiary of Public listed Company 
w.e.f. 9th February, 2022 the applicable provisions of 
the Securities and Exchange Board of India (Listing 
Obligations and Disclosure Requirement) Regulations, 
2015 are complied with.
(vi)	 I have also verified systems and mechanism which 
are in place to ensure Compliance of other applicable 
General Laws viz. Tax Laws, SEZ Rules, Labour Laws etc. 
(in addition to aforementioned Laws applicable to the 
Company) and I have also relied on the representation 
made by the Company and its Officers in respect of 
systems and mechanism formed / followed by the 
Company for compliances of other applicable Acts, 
Laws and Regulations as applicable to the Company 
and found adequate compliance of the same.
I have also examined the compliance with the applicable 
clauses of the Secretarial Standards issued by the Institute 
of Company Secretaries of India.
Based on aforesaid information provided by the Company, I 
report that during the financial year under report, save and 
except below, the Company has complied with the provisions 
of the aforementioned Acts including applicable provisions 
of the Companies Act, 2013 and Rules, Regulations, 
Guidelines, Standards etc. mentioned hereinabove.
I further report that:
The Board of Directors of the Company is duly constituted 
with combination of Executive Directors and Non-Executive 
Directors.
The changes in the composition of Board of Directors that 
took place during the period under review were carried out 
in compliance with the provisions of the Companies Act 
2013.
For the Financial Year ended 31st March, 2026, the company 
is material subsidiary of Ceinsys Tech Limited (Holding 
Company).
Adequate notice was given to all Directors prior to scheduling 
the Board Meetings and Agenda along with detailed notes to 
Agenda were sent at least seven days in advance (and with 
the consent of directors at short notice in case of urgent 
matters) and adequate system exist for the Board Members 
to seek and obtain further information and clarifications on 
the agenda items before the meeting and for meaningful 
participation at the meetings.
It is noted that majority decision is carried through and 
proper system is in place which facilitates / ensure to 
capture and record, the dissenting member’s views, if any, 
as part of the Minutes.
Based on representation made by the Company and 
its Officers to me explaining the internal systems and 
mechanism established by the Company which ensures 
compliances of other Acts, Laws and Regulations applicable 
to the Company, I report that there are adequate systems 
and processes in the Company commensurate with the

ANNUAL REPORT ON CORPORATE SOCIAL RESPONSIBILITY (CSR) ACTIVITIES
1.	
A brief outline of the Company’s CSR Policy:
As an integral part of our commitment to good corporate citizenship, your company believes in actively contributing 
in improvement of the quality of life of people in communities it serves, giving preference to local areas around our 
business operations. Towards achieving long-term stakeholder value creation, your company shall always continue to 
respect the interests of and be responsive towards our key stakeholders–the communities, especially those belonging 
to socially and economically backward groups, the underprivileged and marginalized sections of society.
The Company aims at enhancing its efforts to build on its tradition of social responsibility to empower people and 
deepen its social engagements. For the purpose of focusing Ceinsys CSR in a continued and effective manner, the 
following thrust areas have been identified:
i.	
promote education;
ii.	
health and welfare;
iii.	
community well being;
iv.	
promote gender equality and empower women;
v.	
reduce child mortality and improve maternal health;
vi.	
ensure environmental sustainability;
vii.	 enhance vocational skills on employment;
viii.	 social business projects;
ix.	
Eradicating extreme hunger and poverty
x.	
such other matters as may be decided from time to time, and also may include activities which are connected to 
the company’s core activities and fruitful in the long run.

Annexure-7
CONSERVATION OF ENERGY, RESEARCH AND DEVELOPMENT, TECHNOLOGY ABSORPTION, 
FOREIGN EXCHANGE EARNINGS AND OUTGO
[Particulars pursuant to the Companies (Accounts) Rules, 2014]
CONSERVATION OF ENERGY
The company has procured various energy saving devices and systems, which help in conserving energy. Ceinsys provides 
Information Technology enabled services and hence requires minimal energy consumption and does not use motive power. 
However, every effort is made to ensure that energy efficient equipment is used to avoid wastage and conserve energy, as 
far as possible.
Active measures taken by us towards energy conservation and carbon footprint reduction include using technology to 
monitor and control electricity consumption of air conditioning and other related equipment, use of energy efficient light 
bulbs, using technology for switching off computer monitors etc.
The company undertakes regular reviews of energy requirements and consumption patterns, with action plans for effective 
utilization of power, during peak and non-peak seasons. The company also undertake continuous education and awareness 
programs among all employees on energy conservation measures that can be adopted at individual levels, to help conserve 
power and energy.
The company has reduced excessive illumination levels to standard levels LED lighting in its entire premises and uses four /
five-star air conditioners. Your company has continued to maximize the use of energy efficient flat monitors, energy efficient 
air-conditioning systems. Your company has made efforts to ensure that there is no cool air leakage from its premises and 
have adopted measures to ensure optimum usage of air conditioners throughout its premises. A building automation system 
has been implemented to control working of air conditioners and to make them more power efficient.
Technology Absorption
The Company always adopts new technologies in its Business.
Research and Development
The Company being in IT and related services, continued to carry out Research and Development activity in its routine 
course, however there was no specific expenditure incurred on it.
Foreign Exchange Earnings and Outgo
Foreign Exchange Earnings	 : 	
` 2,619 Lakhs
Foreign Exchange Outgo	
:	
` 245 Lakhs
Due to minimal foreign exchange exposure, the company has not entered into any hedging arrangements.

MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION AND ANALYSIS
ECONOMIC OVERVIEW
Indian Economy
The Indian economy demonstrated resilience during 
FY2025–26 despite a challenging global environment 
characterised by geopolitical tensions, uncertainty in 
international trade, volatility in energy markets and uneven 
growth across major economies.
According to provisional estimates released by the Ministry 
of Statistics and Programme Implementation, India’s real 
Gross Domestic Product grew by 7.7% in FY2025–26, 
with fourth-quarter real GDP growth estimated at 7.8%. 
This represents a stronger outcome than the 7.4% growth 
estimated earlier in the Economic Survey and reinforces 
India’s position among the faster-growing major economies.
Domestic demand remained an important foundation for 
economic activity, supported by consumption, investment 
and continued government expenditure on infrastructure. 
At the same time, structural trends including urbanisation, 
formalisation, manufacturing investment and increasing 
adoption of digital technologies continued to broaden the 
economy’s growth base.
Key Factors Shaping India’s Economic Outlook
•	
Infrastructure-led Growth: Sustained public 
investment in roads, railways, urban development, 
water systems, power networks and logistics remains 
an important economic growth driver. Increasingly, 
infrastructure expenditure is moving beyond physical 
asset creation towards integrated planning, digital 
monitoring and lifecycle asset management, creating 
a broader ecosystem around engineering and 
technology-enabled infrastructure delivery.
•	
Domestic Consumption and Investment: Private 
consumption continued to provide a significant 
foundation for growth. The Economic Survey estimated 
private final consumption expenditure at approximately 
61.5% of GDP, while gross fixed capital formation was 
estimated to have grown by approximately 7.8% during 
FY2025–26. India’s large domestic market, rising 
urbanisation and improving connectivity continue 
to support demand across consumer, services and 
infrastructure-related sectors.
•	
Manufacturing and Formalisation: Continued 
investment in manufacturing capacity, supply-
chain development and industrial infrastructure is 
supporting greater integration of the Indian economy 
with domestic and international value chains. 
Formalisation, digital tax systems and technology-led 
business processes are also contributing to greater 
transparency and productivity across sectors.
•	
Digital Transformation: India’s digital economy 
continues to expand through digital public 
infrastructure, cloud platforms, artificial intelligence, 
digital payments and data-driven governance. 
Technologies such as Geographic Information Systems 
(GIS), Internet of Things (IoT), artificial intelligence and 
advanced analytics are increasingly being incorporated 
into physical infrastructure, manufacturing, 
transportation and utility operations rather than 
remaining confined to technology-led industries.
•	
Macroeconomic Resilience: India continues to possess 
important buffers against external volatility, including 
substantial foreign-exchange reserves, relatively 
moderate inflation, predominantly rupee-denominated 
public debt, diversified trade relationships and a broadly 
resilient financial system. These factors provide some 
protection against international commodity, financial 
and geopolitical shocks.
•	
FY2026–27 Growth Outlook: The forward outlook 
remains positive, although credible forecasts show a 
wider range than in the preceding year. The Economic 
Survey projected real GDP growth of approximately 
6.8–7.2% for FY2026–27, while the World Bank’s 
April 2026 India Development Update projected 
approximately 6.6%, reflecting more recent energy-
price and supply-side risks. India therefore enters 
FY2026–27 from a strong base following 7.7% growth 
in FY2025–26, although global trade, commodity and 
geopolitical conditions remain important variables.
Global Economy
The global economy remained resilient during FY2025–26 
but continued to operate within an uncertain environment 
shaped by geopolitical conflicts, trade-policy changes, 
energy-price volatility and divergent monetary-policy 
conditions.
Forecasts from leading multilateral institutions illustrate 
this uncertainty. The International Monetary Fund’s July 
2026 outlook projected global growth of approximately 
3.0% in 2026 and 3.4% in 2027, while the World Bank’s June 
2026 Global Economic Prospects projected materially lower 
growth of approximately 2.5% in 2026 and 2.8% in 2027. 
Rather than indicating a uniform global recovery, these 
differences reflect significant uncertainty around trade, 
energy markets, geopolitical developments and financial 
conditions.
Key Factors Shaping the Global Economic Outlook
1.	
Geopolitical and Trade Uncertainty: Ongoing 
geopolitical conflicts, trade restrictions and shifts in 
international economic relationships continue to affect 
supply chains, investment decisions and commodity 
markets. Energy security has also re-emerged as 
an important consideration for governments and 
businesses.
2.	
Monetary and Financial Conditions: Major central 
banks continue to balance inflation management with 
the need to support economic activity. Differences in 
monetary-policy trajectories can affect capital flows, 
borrowing costs, currency movements and investment 
sentiment, particularly in emerging economies.
3.	
AI and Technology Investment: Artificial intelligence 
has emerged as one of the most important global 
investment themes. Demand for semiconductors, 
high-performance computing, data centres, cloud 
infrastructure and digital platforms is increasing 
alongside broader adoption of AI in engineering, 
manufacturing, logistics and professional services.
4.	
Energy Transition and Energy Security: Investment 
in renewable energy, storage, transmission 
infrastructure, electric mobility and industrial 
decarbonisation continues to increase. At the same 
time, geopolitical developments have reinforced the 
importance of reliability and security of energy supply, 
increasing the need for resilient and flexible energy 
systems.
5.	
Infrastructure Modernisation: Physical infrastructure 
is becoming increasingly integrated with digital 
systems. Transportation networks, utilities, industrial 
assets and cities are adopting sensors, GIS, automation, 
digital engineering platforms and analytics to improve 
planning, execution and operational efficiency.
6.	
Emerging-market Conditions: Emerging markets 
continue to provide important sources of global growth 
but remain exposed to financing costs, currency 
volatility and energy-price movements. Countries 
with large domestic markets, diversified economic 
structures and substantial infrastructure programmes 
may be comparatively better positioned to absorb 
external shocks.
Overall, the global environment entering FY2026–27 may 
be characterised as resilient but uncertain, with technology 
investment, infrastructure modernisation and energy 
transition providing structural growth drivers while trade 
disputes, geopolitical instability and financing conditions 
remain significant risks.
GOVERNMENT POLICY, INFRASTRUCTURE AND 
PUBLIC CAPITAL EXPENDITURE
India’s infrastructure investment cycle remains an important 
driver of economic activity across construction, engineering, 
transportation, utilities, geospatial technologies and digital 
project management. The scale of public investment is 
increasingly being complemented by greater emphasis on 
integrated planning, technology-enabled monitoring and 
lifecycle asset management.
Key Infrastructure and Policy Developments
•	
Union Government Capital Expenditure: The Union 
Budget for FY2026–27 provides for Central Government 
capital expenditure of approximately `12.22 lakh 
crore. Including grants to states for creation of capital 
assets, effective capital expenditure is budgeted at 
approximately `17.15 lakh crore, equivalent to around 
4.4% of GDP. This provides continued support to large-
scale infrastructure investment across sectors.
•	
Transportation Infrastructure: Public expenditure 
continues across railways, highways, metro systems, 
ports and multimodal logistics. Government information 
associated with the FY2026–27 Budget indicates 
capital expenditure of approximately `2.93 lakh crore 
for Indian Railways, alongside investments in other 
forms of transport infrastructure. Such programmes 
create requirements not only for construction but also 
for engineering design, corridor planning, surveying 
and mapping, construction monitoring and long-term 
asset management.
•	
PM GatiShakti: The PM GatiShakti National Master Plan 
has emerged as an important GIS-based architecture 
for coordinated infrastructure planning. Government 
reporting during 2026 indicated integration across 58 
ministries and departments and more than 3,200 data 
layers. By February 2026, the Network Planning Group 
had evaluated 352 projects with an aggregate value of 
approximately `16.10 lakh crore.
•	
National Geospatial Mission: Announced in the 
Union Budget 2025–26 with an initial allocation 
of `100 crore, the National Geospatial Mission is 
intended to strengthen India’s foundational geospatial 
infrastructure and data ecosystem. Leveraging the 
PM GatiShakti framework, the Mission is envisaged 
to support the modernisation of land records, urban 
planning and the design of infrastructure projects. Its 
proposed components include modernisation of the 
National Geodetic Reference Frame, strengthening 
of geospatial data and mapping infrastructure, 
establishment of Geo-ICT infrastructure, capacity 
building and geospatial technology development. 
The Mission also envisages development of higher-
resolution national elevation and imagery datasets, 
strengthening the foundation for more accurate 
infrastructure planning, land administration and data-
driven governance.
•	
Digitalisation of Infrastructure Planning: Increasing 
use of spatial data in infrastructure planning illustrates 
a transition from fragmented project information 
towards integrated digital decision-making. Asset 
owners increasingly require information relating to land, 
utilities, transport networks, environmental conditions, 
existing infrastructure and project dependencies to be 
considered together. This is increasing the relevance 
of enterprise GIS, spatial data infrastructure, remote 
sensing, LiDAR, Building Information Modelling (BIM),
